“Lester is not to blame, sir; I made him go out of the window, and I held the rope to let him down.”

===Chinese===
没有回音。

“你要是不回答我的问题，我就只好鞭你；跟你同去的还有谁？”

莱斯特先向布拉什投去哀求的目光，又望向亚历山大。亚历克正往本子上写那句话；可一听见灰胡子威胁要鞭打莱斯特，他没写完最后一个词就站了起来。所有的眼睛都转向他，学生们中间起了一阵骚动。

“什么事，亚历山大，”灰胡子问，“这件离奇的表演你知道些什么？”

“莱斯特没有错，先生；是我叫他从窗口出去的，也是我拽着绳子放他下去的。”

===Original===
“And I helped him to do it,” came from a voice in another part of the room; it was Brush, who had silently risen; “we compelled the two﻿—we compelled Lester to go out of the window.”

“You said there were two boys who were going out of the window, who was the other?” asked Graybeard, determined to find out all the participants in the mischief.

Those of us who knew, looked toward Joel; an expression of fear stole over his face and he anxiously awaited Brush’s answer.

“I did not say that, sir,” he replied; “Lester was going down alone.”

Joel gave a sigh of relief.

===Chinese===
“这事我也帮了忙，”屋子另一头传来一个声音；是布拉什，他早已悄无声息地站起来；“是我们逼的这两个——是我们逼莱斯特从窗口出去的。”

“你方才说有两个男孩要出窗，另一个是谁？”灰胡子问，决意把恶作剧的参与者一个不落地查出来。

我们知情的人都朝乔尔望去；他脸上掠过一丝惧色，焦急地等着布拉什的回答。

“我没那么说，先生，”他答道；“莱斯特是一个人下去的。”

乔尔松了一大口气。

===Original===
“What made you force the boy to go out of the window; where were you sending him?”

“We were sending him to the village.”

“What were you sending him to the village for?”

“I refuse to answer,” was the bold reply.

Graybeard, seeing that there would be no use in questioning Brush, turned to Alexander and asked, “What were you sending Lester to the village for?”

===Chinese===
“你们干吗强迫这孩子出窗；你们要打发他去哪儿？”

“我们打发他去村里。”

“打发他去村里做什么？”

“我拒绝回答。”回答得斩钉截铁。

灰胡子见再问布拉什也无益，转向亚历山大，问：“你们打发莱斯特去村里做什么？”

===Original===
“I was sending him to go there and return.”

“Alexander, I want no foolishness; tell me what you were sending Lester to the village for?”

“I refuse to answer.”

“This abusing of smaller boys by the large ones, and making them do things that are improper, must be stopped; it has gone far enough. Lester, you may take your seat. Frank, take this knife and get me two good hickory switches. Do you know a hickory-tree?”

“Yes, sir,” I answered, as I took the knife. I knew every kind of tree growing around the school, and I had a suspicion that Graybeard did not know the difference between a hickory sapling and some others. I cut two formidable-looking switches of linden, closely resembling hickory. I had time to fully doctor only one of the switches, by driving the knife-blade deep into the wood every two or three inches. When I entered the schoolroom, Graybeard took a glance at the switches, then said:

===Chinese===
“我打发他去了又回来。”

“亚历山大，不许胡闹；告诉我，你们打发莱斯特去村里做什么？”

“我拒绝回答。”

“大孩子这样欺负小孩子、逼他们干不体面的事，必须刹住；闹得够过了。莱斯特，回座吧。弗兰克，拿这把刀去给我砍两根结实的山核桃枝。你认得山核桃树吗？”

“认得，先生。”我接过刀答道。学校四周长的每一种树我都认得，而且我疑心灰胡子根本分不清山核桃树苗和别的什么树。我砍了两根样子吓人的椴树枝——同山核桃极像。来得及做手脚的只有一根：每隔两三寸，我就把刀刃深深楔进木头。我走进教室，灰胡子瞥了一眼那两根枝条，说：

===Original===
“Alexander and Brush will step to my desk and take off their coats.”

The two boys stood in their shirt sleeves; I kept watch of Graybeard’s eyes, and saw that he was going to take Brush first; so when he was ready I handed him the fully doctored switch.

“Is that hickory?” he asked, trying it on the air; “I suspect it isn’t.”

I made no reply.

“Stand in the middle of the floor,” said Graybeard to Brush.

===Chinese===
“亚历山大和布拉什到讲台前来，脱掉外衣。”

两个男孩穿着衬衫站定；我盯着灰胡子的眼睛，看出他要先拿布拉什开刀；于是等他准备好，我把那根做足了手脚的枝条递了过去。

“这是山核桃吗？”他问，朝空中试了一记；“我看不是。”

我不答话。

“站到屋子中间来。”灰胡子对布拉什说。

===Original===
He did so. Graybeard brought down the stick heavily on Brush’s shoulders, an inch of the sapling broke; then he struck faster and faster, and at each stroke a piece flew off. Brush stood with clenched fists, determined not to show any flinching; but we could see that he felt keenly the blows. He went to his desk, and buried his face in his arms.

“I am afraid this isn’t hickory,” said Graybeard, throwing on the floor the stump of the switch. “I know this one is,” and he dealt blow after blow on the broad shoulders of Alexander, who gave no sign of pain. The boy stood unmoved, every muscle relaxed, even his hands were open, showing no emotion whatever. The stick was worn out, and Graybeard threw the stump on the floor.

Aleck put on his coat, then, with head uplifted and unfaltering steps, went to his desk, took his pen, and completed the unfinished word of the motto.

===Chinese===
他照办了。灰胡子把枝条沉沉抽在布拉什肩上，树苗断了一寸；他越抽越快，每抽一记就飞掉一截。布拉什攥紧拳头站着，打定主意不露一点怯；可我们看得出这一下下他痛得真切。他回到座位，把脸埋进臂弯里。

“恐怕这不是山核桃，”灰胡子说着，把枝条的残桩扔在地上。“这根我认得是，”说着朝亚历山大宽阔的肩膀一记接一记抽去，男孩毫无痛色。他站得纹丝不动，浑身肌肉松弛，连手都摊开着，什么情绪也不露。枝条抽烂了，灰胡子把残桩扔在地上。

亚历克穿上外衣，昂着头、步子一丝不乱地走回座位，拿起笔，把格言里那个没写完的词补全了。

## VII The Splinter, the Thorn, and the Rib / 木刺、荆棘与肋骨

===Original===
“Oh! oh! oh! Aunt, that hurts. Oh!”

“Keep still, now, keep still! You have a big stick in your toe, and I must take it out. If you keep pulling like that, I might run the point of this awl into your foot.”

I lay flat on my back on the ground with my sore foot in the lap of this good woman whom I called Aunt, while she probed the wound to withdraw a splinter. After considerable wincing on my part, the cause of my agony was removed and held to view. The splinter was long and very large; the relief was great, and already I felt as though I could walk without limping. The kind woman took from her workbag a bit of root, chewed it, and put it on my sore toe; then she bandaged the foot with a piece of white cloth which also came from the handy bag.

My Aunt laid the splinter on a piece of wood and cut it into fine bits, just as I had seen men cut tobacco for smoking. “Now,” said she, as she scattered the bits in every direction, “that thing cannot do any more harm. But what is this?” she asked, holding the old bandage up between the tips of her thumb and index finger of her right hand, and in her left the bit of pork that had been tied on my toe.

===Chinese===
“哦！哦！哦！婶婶，疼啊。哦！”

“别动，这就别动！你脚趾里扎了根大刺，我得把它挑出来。你要是老这么挣，我这锥子尖说不定就戳进你脚里了。”

我仰面平躺在地上，那只疼脚搁在这位我唤作婶婶的好心妇人膝上，由她探着伤口往外挑木刺。我龇牙咧嘴了好一阵，那让我受罪的祸根终于被拔了出来，举给我看。刺又长又粗；一身轻松，我已经觉得自己走路可以不瘸了。好心妇人从针线袋里掏出一小块树根，嚼了嚼，敷在我疼的脚趾上；又用一块白布把脚裹起来——布也是那只顺手百宝袋里的。

婶婶把木刺搁在一块木头上，切成细末，就像我见过男人们切烟丝那样。“好了，”她一面把碎末四下撒开，一面说，“这东西再也害不了人了。可这是什么？”她问，右手拇指和食指尖上捏着旧绷带举起来，左手捏着先前绑在我脚趾上的那块猪肉。

===Original===
“Why, Aunt,” I replied, “that thing in your right hand is the old bandage, and that in your left is the pig-fat that was put on my toe.”

“Why did they put pig-fat on your poor sore toe; who put it on? Bah! It’s nasty!” she exclaimed, as she threw it away as far as she could.

“The white woman who takes care of the children at the school put it on to draw the splinter out.”

“To draw the splinter out!” she repeated in a tone of contempt. Then she tossed up her fine head, gave shouts of laughter, and said between the paroxysms; “Oh! this is funny! This is funny! Your White-chests might as well hitch a bit of pig-fat to their wagon and expect it to draw a load up the hill! And how long has this pig-fat been tied on your foot?”

“About four days.”

“Without bathing the foot and renewing the bandages?”

“Yes.”

“If this white woman takes as much care of the other children as she has of you﻿—I’m sorry for them. No children of mine should be placed under her care﻿—if I had any.”

My Aunt gathered her awl, knife, and other little things into her workbag; I looked all about to see if any boys were watching, then I put my arms around her dear neck and kissed her.

===Chinese===
“哟，婶婶，”我答道，“你右手那个是旧绷带，左手那个是绑在我脚趾上的猪油。”

“干吗往你可怜的疼脚趾上绑猪油？谁绑的？呸！脏死了！”她一面嚷，一面把它使劲扔得远远的。

“是学校里照看孩子的那个白人女人绑的，说是要把刺拔出来。”

“把刺拔出来！”她轻蔑地重复了一遍。随即昂起她那好看的头，放声大笑，笑得一阵一阵地说：“哦！笑死人了！笑死人了！你们白胸人还不如拴块猪油在车前，指望它把一车货拉上山呢！这块猪油在你脚上绑了多久？”

“大概四天。”

“也没洗脚、没换绷带？”

“没有。”

“这白人女人要是照顾别的孩子也这么照顾你——我替他们难过。我的孩子可不能交给她照管——要是我有孩子的话。”

婶婶把锥子、刀子和别的小物件收拾进针线袋；我四下看了看有没有男孩在瞧，然后搂住她可爱的脖子亲了亲。

===Original===
“Are you going to see my mother today?” When she answered yes, I said, “Tell her to come and see me﻿—very soon.”

“I will; but don’t keep her running over here all the time,” and she started to go. She had not gone very far when she turned and shouted to me, “Wash your foot tomorrow morning and turn the bandage over. You will be well in a day or two.”

A boy passing by cried out, “Bell has rung!” and I limped into the schoolroom to attend the afternoon session.

When school was out, Lester suggested that we go on the hill to sit and talk. Turning to me, he asked if I could walk as far as that; I assured him that I could, so I hobbled along with the boys up the hill. We found a beautiful grassy spot, and three of us﻿—Lester, Warren, and I﻿—lay down and looked up into the deep blue sky. Brush sat near by, carving a horse’s head out of a piece of oak. Clouds lazily floated far above.

“Say, Lester,” I called, “you take that one that looks like a buffalo; Warren, you take that one that is shaped like a bear; and I will take this one that’s like a man smoking a pipe. Now, let’s rub them out!”

So, fixing our eyes upon the clouds, we began rubbing the palms of our hands together.

===Chinese===
“你今天去看我母亲吗？”她答说去，我便说：“叫她来看看我——很快。”

“我会说的；可别叫她一趟一趟老往这儿跑，”说着她动身走了。没走多远她回头朝我喊：“明天早晨把脚洗一洗，绷带翻个面。一两天就好了。”

一个路过的男孩喊：“打过铃了！”我一瘸一拐进了教室，上下午的课。

放学后，莱斯特提议上山去坐着聊天。他转头问我能不能走到那儿；我向他保证能，就一瘸一拐跟男孩们上了山。我们寻到一块漂亮草地，三个人——莱斯特、沃伦和我——躺下仰望深蓝的天空。布拉什坐在近旁，用一块橡木雕一颗马头。云在远远的高处懒懒地飘。

“哎，莱斯特，”我喊，“你认那朵像野牛的；沃伦，你认那朵像熊的；我认这朵像人抽烟斗的。现在，咱们把它们擦掉！”

于是我们盯住云，开始搓起手掌来。

===Original===
“Mine is getting smaller, right away, now!” cried Warren.

“Mine too!” echoed Lester.

Brush gave us a look of disgust, and said, “Boys, I think you are the biggest fools I ever saw﻿—rubbing out clouds, the idea!”

But we rubbed away, and paid no attention to the contemptuous glances our friend gave us. My hands began to come down lower and lower; and then I felt myself rising from the ground, higher and higher I went, just like a big bird, and suddenly landed on a heavy black cloud. I looked down; there were the boys still rubbing away, and Brush still carving. I could see the winding river far below and the birds flitting about. I wondered what it all meant. I felt the cloud moving away with me; the boys were growing smaller and smaller, and I noticed that I was passing over the Indian village. Where is the cloud going with me, and will it ever stop? I heard a sound that seemed familiar to me﻿—is it a bell? Could there be bells in the cloud? I asked myself.

“Wake up, you fools! Supper-bell has rung! Rubbing out clouds, were you!” said Brush, in derisive tones.

===Chinese===
“我的那朵正在变小，眼看着小了！”沃伦喊。

“我的也是！”莱斯特应和。

布拉什嫌恶地瞅了我们一眼，说：“伙计们，我看你们是我见过的最大的傻瓜——擦掉云，亏想得出！”

可我们照搓不误，全不理会朋友投来的白眼。我的手越垂越低；随后我感到自己离开了地面，越升越高，像一只大鸟，忽然落在一朵沉沉的乌云上。我朝下望；男孩们还在搓，布拉什还在雕。我看得见远远下方蜿蜒的河和飞来飞去的鸟。我不知道这一切是什么意思。我感到云载着我移动；男孩们越来越小，我发觉自己正飘过印第安人的村子。云要把我带到哪儿去，它会停吗？我听见一个耳熟的声音——是铃吗？云里难道会有铃？我问自己。

“醒醒，你们这些傻瓜！晚饭铃都响过了！擦云呢是吧！”布拉什嘲弄地说。

===Original===
Warren sat up, blinking his eyes, and asked, “Where are we?”

That night, when the boys had settled down in their beds and Graybeard had gone downstairs, Edwin asked, “Boys, where’ve you been this afternoon? You came to supper late; Graybeard looked hard at you.”

“We’ve been up the hill,” I answered; “I told the boys to hurry along and leave me; but they wouldn’t.”

“Who was that Indian woman talking to you before dinnertime?”

“That was my aunt; she saw me when she was going by, and she made me sit down and she looked at my foot. She took a great big splinter out of my toe. My! it hurt.”

“You’re going to get well now. Why didn’t you put that splinter in some buffalo hair, then ’t would’ve turned into a baby.”

===Chinese===
沃伦坐起来，眨着眼问：“我们在哪儿？”

那天夜里，男孩们都上了床、灰胡子也下楼之后，埃德温问：“伙计们，你们下午去哪儿了？晚饭来晚了；灰胡子狠狠瞅了你们。”

“上山去了，”我答道；“我叫伙伴们只管走别等我；可他们不肯。”

“饭前跟你说话的那个印第安女人是谁？”

“是我婶婶；她路过瞧见了我，叫我坐下，看了看我的脚。她从我脚趾里挑出一根老大的木刺。天哪！疼着呢。”

“你这就要好了。你干吗不把那根刺搁进野牛毛里，那它就会变成个娃娃了。”

===Original===
“Nonsense!” said Brush, “who ever heard of such a thing.”

“There’s a story like that,” replied Edwin.

“Tell that story! tell that story!” cried the boys in chorus.

“But you don’t listen; you go to sleep, or you ask fool questions and stop me.”

“We won’t stop you; we’re going to lie awake.”

“All right. I’ll tell you that story. Say ‘ong!’ pretty soon, then I’ll know you’re awake.”

We all snuggled down, then in chorus cried, “Ong!” and Edwin began:

===Chinese===
“胡说！”布拉什说，“谁听说过这种事。”

“有这么个故事。”埃德温答。

“讲那个故事！讲那个故事！”男孩们齐声喊。

“可你们又不听；不是睡觉，就是问些傻问题打断我。”

“我们不打断你；我们睁着眼睛躺着。”

“好吧。我给你们讲那个故事。过一会儿就喊一声‘ong！’，我就知道你们醒着。”

我们都缩进被窝，齐声喊：“Ong！”埃德温开讲：

===Original===
“ ’Way long time ago, four brothers lived on earth. Good hunters, they shoot straight, kill deer, buffalo, elk, and all kinds of animals. They got plenty of meat and skins. One night, the youngest man came home very lame; his foot was all swelled up; he had to use his bow for a cane, and he was groaning, groaning all the time. He lay down and was real sick, one, two, three days. The other men, they went hunting. When they were gone, the youngest man got up, took his knife, cut open his toe, and took out a big thorn, a great big﻿—”

Whack! whack! whack! Quick as a flash the boys put their feet against the footboard and pulled the bedclothes taut so that the rest of the blows fell harmless upon us. We had been surprised by Graybeard. Edwin, in his earnestness, and in his belief that a foreign language can be better understood when spoken loudly, had been shouting his story in a voice that reached Graybeard and woke him up. After warning us against loud talking, the old man went downstairs as stealthily as he had come.

“Well, boys,” said Brush, “that came like a cyclone, didn’t it?”

We all agreed that it did.

===Chinese===
“老早老早以前，地上住着四兄弟。都是好猎手，箭射得直，打的着鹿、野牛、麋鹿，什么兽都打得着。他们有的是肉和皮子。一天夜里，最小的那个男人一瘸一拐回了家；脚肿得老高；只好拄着弓当拐棍，一路哼哼唧唧个不停。他躺下了，病得不轻，一天，两天，三天。别的男人出去打猎。他们一走，最小的男人就爬起来，拿刀割开脚趾，取出一根大刺，一根老大的——”

啪！啪！啪！说时迟那时快，男孩们齐齐把脚蹬住床尾板，把被子绷得紧紧的，后面落下来的几下都打不到我们身上。我们被灰胡子打了个措手不及。埃德温讲得入神，又坚信外国话喊得越响越听得懂，把嗓门放得连灰胡子都被吵醒了。老头子告诫我们不许大声说话之后，又像来时那样蹑手蹑脚地下楼去了。

“嗐，伙计们，”布拉什说，“这一阵来得跟龙卷风似的，是不是？”

我们都说像。

===Original===
“Frank, did he hurt your foot?” asked Warren.

“No, the boys kept the quilt up, so he couldn’t hit me.”

“What did I say last?” asked Edwin.

“You said,” I reminded him, “that he cut open his toe and took out a big thorn.”

“Oh, yes,” he continued; “he took out a big thorn, a great big thorn. He wanted to show it to his brothers, so he pulled out some buffalo hair from his robe and put the thorn inside and laid it away, way back in the middle of the tent. Then he went after some water to wash his foot. When he was coming back, he heard something crying like everything; not like raccoon, not like any kind of bird or animal, something different. He stood still and listened; it sounded like coming from inside the tent! So he went slow, easy, and looked in the tent; there was something moving and crying loud. Then the young man went inside the tent, and he saw a baby, a little girl baby, and no thorn. He knew that thorn had turned into a girl baby, crying like everything. The young man was very glad; he danced on his one well foot; he took up the girl baby in his big arms and moved like a tree when the wind blows, and he sang soft, and the girl baby shut her eyes and went to sleep, e‑a‑s‑y﻿—just like you!”

“No! We ain’t asleep. Go on.”

===Chinese===
“弗兰克，他打着你的脚了吗？”沃伦问。

“没有，男孩们把被子高高撑着，他打不着我。”

“我方才讲到哪儿了？”埃德温问。

“你说，”我提醒他，“他割开脚趾，取出一根大刺。”

“哦，对，”他接着讲；“他取出一根大刺，一根老大的刺。他想给哥哥们瞧瞧，就从皮袍上扯下几撮野牛毛，把刺裹在里头，搁在帐篷正中最里面的地方。然后他去打水洗脚。回来的时候，听见什么东西哭得惊天动地；不像浣熊，不像什么鸟儿兽儿，是另一种哭法。他站住细听；像是帐篷里传出来的！他就放轻脚步，慢慢凑过去，朝帐篷里一望；有什么东西在动，哭得响。年轻的男人走进帐篷，看见一个娃娃，一个小女娃，刺没有了。他知道那根刺变成了个女娃，哭得惊天动地。年轻的男人欢喜坏了；他用那只没病的脚跳起舞来；张开两条大胳膊抱起女娃，像风吹的树那样摇啊摇，轻轻唱着歌，小女娃阖上眼睛睡着了，睡得香——香的——就跟你们一个样！”

“没有！我们没睡。接着讲。”

===Original===
“Well, those big brothers came home, and they were all very glad. They took the girl baby all round. Then the oldest brother, he said, ‘She is going to be our sister. I wish she would grow right up and run round the tent.’ Then he lifted her four times, and the girl baby grew quick, and ran round the tent, talking. Then another brother, he said, ‘I wish my sister would grow up and get big enough to go after water.’ Then he lifted the little girl four times, and she got big enough to go after water. Then the next one, he said, ‘I wish my sister would grow big enough to make moccasins and cook and make lots of things.’ Then he lifted her four times, and the girl grew right up and knew how to make lots of things. Then the youngest man, he said, ‘I wish my sister grown up woman now.’ Then he lifted her four times, and she was a big woman right away. So in one night that thorn girl baby grew up, and she was the first woman.”

“Why!” said Brush, “that’s just like the Bible story of Adam and Eve. You remember it says, that Adam was the first man God made, and He put him in a big garden full of flowers and trees. He told him he could eat everything there except the berries of only one tree, and He showed him that tree. God made Adam go to sleep, and then He cut open his side and took out one rib, and out of that bone He made a woman, and He named her Eve.”

“Did He whittle that rib bone just like you whittle a piece of wood and make men, and horses, and dogs, and other things?” asked Lester.

===Chinese===
“后来呀，那几个哥哥打猎回来了，一个个欢喜得很。他们抱着小女娃挨个传看。大哥说：‘她要做我们的妹妹。真盼她快点长大，能绕着帐篷跑。’他把女娃举起来举了四回，女娃一下子就长大了，绕着帐篷跑，还会说话。二哥说：‘盼着我妹妹快长大，大到能去打水。’他把小姑娘举了四回，她就长到能去打水了。三哥说：‘盼着我妹妹长得会做鹿皮鞋、会做饭、会做好多好多东西。’他把她举了四回，姑娘一下子就长大了，会做好多好多东西。最小的男人说：‘盼着我妹妹这会儿长成大女人。’他把她举了四回，她当场就成了个大女人。就这样，一夜之间，那个刺变的娃娃女娃长大了，她就是世上第一个女人。”

“哎呀！”布拉什说，“这跟圣经里亚当夏娃的故事一模一样。你们记得那上面说，亚当是上帝造的头一个男人，上帝把他安置在一座开满花、长满树的大园子里。告诉他那里样样都能吃，只有一棵树的果子不能碰，还指给他看了那棵树。上帝叫亚当睡着，然后剖开他的肋旁，取出一根肋骨，用那根骨头造了个女人，给她起名叫夏娃。”

“他把那根肋骨削啊削的，就像你削木头做小人、做马、做狗、做别的玩意儿那样吗？”莱斯特问。

===Original===
“Yes, I think He did. Then in that garden there were elephants, and lions, and tigers, and camels, and lots of other animals; but they didn’t eat each other up. God gave Adam the camels to ride, so he wouldn’t get tired. Camels ride easy, easier than a horse. You know a horse goes trot! trot! trot! and makes your stomach ache; but a camel goes just as e‑a‑s‑y, like rocking, like that boat, you know, when we went on the river and the wind blew, and the boat went up and down. Why, you know, the difference is just like this: you ride in a big wagon and it shakes you like everything; you ride in the superintendent’s carriage, and it rides just as easy as anything.”

“How do you know?” broke in Warren. “You never rode a camel, and you never rode in the superintendent’s carriage.”

“Yes, I have too. I’ve ridden in the superintendent’s carriage that time I went to interpret for him down to the big village. I rode with him in his carriage.”

“You boys said you wouldn’t stop my story,” protested Edwin, yawning.

“Say, Brush,” I asked, “when that bone was whittled, and it became Eve, what did she do?”

===Chinese===
“对，我看是。那园子里还有大象、狮子、老虎、骆驼，还有好多别的动物；可它们谁也不吃谁。上帝把骆驼给亚当骑，免得他累。骆驼骑起来舒服，比马舒服。你知道马跑起来颠！颠！颠！颠得你肚子疼；骆驼可走得又稳——又——轻，像摇篮似的，就像那只船，咱们上回坐船过河，风吹着，船一起一伏。嗐，你们知道，差别就像这样：你坐大板车，颠得你要散架；你坐校监的马车，那叫一个平稳。”

“你怎么知道？”沃伦插嘴。“你又没骑过骆驼，也没坐过校监的马车。”

“怎么没坐过。校监那回去大村子里叫我给他传话，我就坐了他的马车。我跟他同车坐的。”

“你们说了不打断我讲故事的。”埃德温打着呵欠抗议。

“哎，布拉什，”我问，“那根骨头削成了人，成了夏娃，后来她怎么样？”

===Original===
“Well, one morning she went down to the creek to swim, and, just as she was going to step into the water by a big willow-tree, she saw a snake in the tree with a man’s head on, and the snake﻿—”

“It wasn’t a snake,” interrupted Warren; “it was the serpent, the Sunday-school teacher said so.”

“Well, it’s the same thing﻿—the snake and the serpent is the same thing.”

“No, they’re not. The serpent is the kind that’s poisonous, like the rattlesnake; and the snake is like those that don’t poison, like the garter-snake and the bull-snake.”

“Brush, go on with your story,” I broke in impatiently. “Don’t mind Warren; he doesn’t know anything!”

“No, he doesn’t. Well, the serpent was Satan, and Sa﻿—”

“How can Satan be a serpent and a snake?” asked Lester. “First you said it was a snake; then you said it was a serpent; now you say it was Satan!”

===Chinese===
“嗐，一天早晨她下河去游泳，刚要在棵大柳树旁下水，看见树上有条蛇，长着个人头，那蛇——”

“那不是蛇，”沃伦打断道；“是古蛇，主日学老师这么说的。”

“嗐，一回事——蛇和古蛇是一回事。”

“不，不是一回事。古蛇是带毒的那一种，像响尾蛇；蛇是不带毒的，像袜带蛇、牛蛇。”

“布拉什，接着讲你的故事，”我不耐烦地插嘴，“别理沃伦；他懂什么！”

“对，他是不懂。那古蛇就是撒旦，撒——”

“撒旦怎么能又是古蛇又是蛇？”莱斯特问。“你先说那是蛇；又说那是古蛇；这会儿又说那是撒旦！”

===Original===
“You boys are bothering my story all the time. I’m going to stop.”

“Go on, Brush,” I urged; “don’t mind those boys; what do they know? They’re all way back in the Second Reader, and you are in the Fifth, and I am in the Third.”

“All right, I’ll go on; I don’t care what they say. Well, the Devil spoke to Eve and said﻿—”

“Your snake has turned into a Devil now,” sneered Edwin. “Boys, why don’t you let me go on with my story; Brush doesn’t know how to tell a story.”

“Yes, I do too. Boys, you don’t know anything; you don’t know that the Devil and Satan and the serpent and the snake are the same thing; they’re all the same. If you would listen when the teacher talks to you in the schoolroom, and when the minister speaks to us in the chapel, you would learn something. All you got to do is to listen, but you don’t. When you are forced to sit still, you go to sleep; and when you are awake you tickle those that are asleep with straws, or stick pins in them. How are you going to learn anything when you do like that? You must listen; that’s what I’m doing. I want to know all about these things so I can be a preacher when I get big. I’m going to wear a long black coat, and a vest that buttons up to the throat, and I’m going to wear a white collar, and a pair of boots that squeaks and reaches to my knees, and﻿—”

“Edwin, go on with your story, I want to hear that,” called Warren.

“He’s asleep,” said I.

===Chinese===
“你们老是搅我的故事。我不讲了。”

“讲下去，布拉什，”我催他；“别理那两个；他们懂什么？他们还在《第二读本》里泡着呢，你都到《第五读本》了，我也在《第三读本》了。”

“好吧，我讲；管他们说什么。那魔鬼就跟夏娃说话，说——”

“你的蛇这会儿又变成魔鬼了，”埃德温讥笑。“伙计们，干吗不让我讲我的故事；布拉什根本不会讲故事。”

“谁不会。伙计们，你们什么都不懂；你们不知道魔鬼、撒旦、古蛇和蛇是一回事；全是一回事。老师在教室里讲、牧师在礼拜堂里讲的时候你们肯听，就长学问了。你们只要听就行，可你们偏不听。逼着你们坐好的时候，你们睡觉；醒着呢，就拿麦秆捅睡觉的，或者拿别针扎人家。你们这样还能学着什么？你们要听；我就是这么做的。我要把这些事统统弄明白，长大了当个传道人。我要穿一件长长的黑外衣，一件扣到脖子的马甲，戴白领子，穿一双吱吱叫、齐膝盖的长靴，还有——”

“埃德温，接着讲你的故事，我想听那个。”沃伦喊。

“他睡着了。”我说。

===Original===
“Only last Sunday,” resumed Brush, “the minister told us that the Devil went about like a roaring lion seeking whom he may de﻿—de﻿—What’s the rest of that word, Frank?”

“Vour.”

“Yes, ’vour, devour. The Devil went about like a roaring lion seeking whom he may devour.”

“Bully for you, Brush!” exclaimed Lester. “That’s good; you didn’t cough big though, like the preacher does.”

“Don’t make fun of the old man, boys, he is here to help us; he wants to do us good.”

“Yes,” answered Warren; “I guess he wanted to do you good last week, when he switched your back for you!”

===Chinese===
“就在上个星期天，”布拉什接着说，“牧师告诉我们，魔鬼如同吼叫的狮子，遍地游行，寻找可吞——吞——那字后头是什么来着，弗兰克？”

“吃。”

“对，吞吃。魔鬼如同吼叫的狮子，遍地游行，寻找可吞吃的人。”

“好样的，布拉什！”莱斯特喊。“讲得真好；就是没像牧师那样咳嗽个大的。”

“别拿老头子打趣，伙计们，他是来帮我们的；他是一心为我们好。”

“是呀，”沃伦答道；“上礼拜他‘为你好’，用枝条抽了你的背，我还记得！”

===Original===
“I think I deserved it.”

“No, you didn’t. You didn’t do anything; you only threw Phil Sheridan down and made his nose bleed.”

“I shouldn’t have done it. I saw a good chance and I did it, and the old man was looking at me. Now, boys, what did the preacher mean when he said the Devil went around like a roaring lion?”

“I s’pose,” said Edwin, “he means the Devil is like some of our big medicine men who can turn themselves into deer and elk, and any kind of animal, and the Devil can change himself into a hungry, howling lion and﻿—”

“And into a Satan,” suggested Lester.

===Chinese===
“我想那是我该受的。”

“不，你不该。你什么也没干；你不过把菲尔·谢里登摔了个跟头，让他出了鼻血。”

“我不该那么干的。我瞧着机会好就干了，偏偏老头子正瞧着我。嗐，伙计们，牧师说魔鬼像吼叫的狮子四处走，到底是什么意思？”

“我猜呀，”埃德温说，“他是说魔鬼就像我们有些大巫师，能把自己变成鹿啊、麋鹿啊，变什么兽都成，魔鬼也能把自己变成一头饿吼的狮子，还有——”

“还能变成撒旦。”莱斯特提示。

===Original===
“And into a serpent,” added Warren.

“Into a snake,” I chimed in.

“And put a man’s head on!” ejaculated Edwin.

“And talk to women when they go swimming!” said Lester, with a laugh.

“There’s no use talking to you boys. I’m going to sleep,” and Brush turned over.

One by one, sleep overcame these boys. Brush made a peculiar noise as he breathed, and Lester puffed away like a steamboat.

A whippoorwill sang in one of the cottonwood-trees near the corner of the house. Fainter and fainter grew the sound, and so the day passed into yesterday, and the morrow began to dawn.

===Chinese===
“还能变成古蛇。”沃伦补充。

“变成蛇。”我插一句。

“还安个人头！”埃德温喊。

“还专在女人去游泳的时候跟她们搭话！”莱斯特笑着说完。

“跟你们这帮家伙说话白费劲。我睡了。”布拉什翻了个身。

男孩们一个个被睡魔降服。布拉什呼吸声很特别，莱斯特则像轮船似的扑扑喷气。

一只三声夜鹰在屋角近旁的一棵三角叶杨上叫。声音越来越轻，就这样，今天成了昨天，明天开始放亮。

## V Warren / 沃伦

===Original===
Brush was a genius as a whittler. He had only one tool, and that was a rusty jackknife with a single broken blade, and that blade was kept sharp almost to the keenness of a razor. He would take a shapeless piece of wood, out here, out there, scrape at one place, then at another, and go through a series of twists and turns of his strong, deft hands, and at last, with a triumphant smile, hold up to view a wooden horse, buffalo, or some other animal. He had just now finished a little plough which he had been carving for some time, and we, the Middle Five, sat in the shade of a tree noisily discussing the accuracy of the work.

“Brush, that’s pretty good, it’s just like the ploughs I’ve seen,” I remarked as I passed the toy to Edwin.

“ ’Tain’t good,” said Edwin, after he had examined it a while. “I think the handles are too straight.”

“This ought to be kind of crooked, come down like this,” put in Lester, indicating with his finger the outline of the beam as it should have been, according to his notion.

Our heads were close together looking at the plough, when a sudden consciousness as of the presence of something disagreeable stole upon us. A sound like the snapping of a twig made us all look up, and there stood Jim, a big boy, one of the worst that ever entered our school, and who had been excluded from all the gangs on account of his vicious, meddlesome disposition. With a contemptuous grin, he passed his eyes from one boy to the other, as though to discern the character of each one. When this unpleasant stare fell upon Warren, he bristled up, gave back a defiant look, and kept it steadily upon the unwelcome visitor. Without relaxing the mirthless smile, so characteristic of him, Jim addressed the boy, “Warren, I just come from the spring, where a lot of boys was talking. I heard Gid say that he could lick you. I told him I’d come and tell you what he said. Then he says, ‘I don’t care, I ain’t ’fraid of him!’ ”

===Chinese===
布拉什是削木头的天才。他只有一件家什，一把锈迹斑斑的单刃折刀，刃还断了，可磨得几乎跟剃刀一样快。他拿起一块没形状的木头，这里削削，那里削削，一处刮一刮，再换一处，那双有力灵巧的手辗转腾挪一番，最后带着得胜的微笑举起给人看——一匹木马、一头木野牛，或别的什么兽。他刚做完一具削了些时日的小犁，我们中间五人坐在树荫下，闹哄哄地评说这活儿的精度。

“布拉什，做得不赖，跟我见过的犁一模一样，”我说着把玩具递给埃德温。

“不赖才怪，”埃德温端详了一阵说。“我看把手太直了。”

“这儿该有点弯，像这样下来，”莱斯特插嘴，用手指比着他心目中犁辕该有的样子。

我们脑袋凑在一起看那具犁，忽然一种有什么讨厌东西在场的预感悄悄爬上心头。咔的一声像折断树枝，我们齐抬头——吉姆（Jim）站在那儿，一个大男孩，是我们学校进过的最坏的坯子之一，因为心术不正、爱管闲事，被所有的帮派拒之门外。他咧着嘴冷笑，目光从一个男孩扫到另一个，像要掂量各人的斤两。这道不受欢迎的目光落到沃伦身上时，沃伦毛发倒竖，回敬了一记挑衅的瞪视，死死盯住这不速之客。吉姆脸上那丝不含笑意的冷笑——他的招牌——纹丝不动，开口对沃伦说：“沃伦，我刚从泉水边来，一帮男孩在那儿说话。我听见吉德（Gid）说他能揍扁你。我告诉他，我要来把他这话捎给你。他就说：‘随便，我才不怕他呢！’”

===Original===
“You go and tell Gid,” said Warren, springing to his feet, “I can lick two like him, and I’ll show him any time he wants me to.”

The mischief-maker had read well the character of Warren, and had won from him the expected reply.

We resumed our examination of the plough thinking that our interview with the talebearer had ended. Jim thrust his hands into his pockets, and walked uneasily about; he came to where little Bob was sitting, and, pulling out a warty hand, he pointed his finger at the boy’s face, making a hissing sound between his teeth. Jim never passed by a chance to tease a smaller boy. Bob put his hands to his face and began crying. We all rose to our feet; Edwin moved forward in a threatening attitude, and said, “Jim, you let that boy alone. What you want to tease him for?”

Jim turned away, looked up into a tree, threw a stone at a bird, and then slowly sauntered off.

We sat down again to resume our talk about Brush’s little plough, but our minds seemed to turn in another direction.

===Chinese===
“你去告诉吉德，”沃伦一跃而起，“像他那样的我一个能揍俩，他什么时候想领教，我随时奉陪。”

挑事的人对沃伦的脾气摸得透透的，果然套出了他想要的回答。

我们接着看那具犁，以为同这搬嘴弄舌的家伙的交道到此为止了。吉姆双手插兜，心神不定地走来走去；他踱到小鲍勃坐着的地方，抽出一只长满疣的手，指头点着孩子的脸，齿缝里嘶嘶作响。吉姆从来不放过作践小男孩的机会。鲍勃捂住脸哭起来。我们全都站起；埃德温摆出威胁的架势逼上前去，说：“吉姆，离那孩子远点。你惹他干什么？”

吉姆转过脸，仰头望着一棵树，朝一只鸟扔了块石头，然后慢慢悠悠荡开了。

我们重新坐下，接着谈布拉什的小犁，可心思好像转了向。

===Original===
“I don’t want Warren to fight Gideon,” said Edwin; “he’s a bad fellow, that Gideon is. He don’t fight fair.”

“But he can’t back out,” spoke up Lester, “and I don’t want him to. I don’t want the rest of the boys to think he’s ’fraid.”

“Warren’s got to fight Gid,” exclaimed Brush. “If he only kept quiet and didn’t say anything when Jim told him what Gid said, it would be all right and no fight; but now everybody knows what Warren said, and he can’t back out without the boys thinking he’s a coward. We will see that Gid fights fair, and, if he don’t, we will thrash his whole gang. Warren can use his arms and fists all right; but he can’t wrestle very good. Frank, you’d better show him some of those new holds.”

Warren and I took several rounds in which I showed him a number of new tricks I had learned from a good wrestler. There was quite an important one of which he was ignorant; I gave him some lessons in that; then we sat down to talk over the challenge again with the rest of the boys.

“I think Warren can throw Gid right easy,” I said; “if he can remember that waist and chin trick, and the way to break it, he can down Gid every time.”

===Chinese===
“我不想让沃伦跟基甸（Gideon）打，”埃德温说；“那家伙坏，基甸坏着呢。他打架不讲规矩。”

“可他退不掉了，”莱斯特开了腔，“我也不想叫他退。我不想叫别的男孩当他是个怕事的。”

“沃伦非跟吉德打不可，”布拉什说。“要是吉姆传话的时候他不吭声、不接茬，那就什么事没有，架也打不成；可如今沃伦的话人人都知道了，他不打就是怯场。咱们盯着点，叫吉德守规矩；他要是不守，咱们把他那一伙全揍扁。沃伦胳膊拳头都使得；就是摔跤差点劲。弗兰克，你把那几手新把式教教他。”

沃伦和我过了几招，我把从一个摔跤好手那里学来的几手新窍门教给了他。有一手顶要紧的他还不会；我给他点拨了几课；然后我们坐下，同大伙儿接着合计这场挑战。

“我看沃伦撂倒吉德轻松，”我说；“只要他记得那手抱腰托下巴，还有破它的法子，他回回都能放倒吉德。”

===Original===
“Remember that!” warned Lester, looking at his brother. “If Gid plays that waist and chin trick, you do just what Frank showed you to do to break it.”

While we were talking, we heard the slapping of bare feet upon the hard ground, and soon a boy appeared before us, imitating the actions of a spirited horse. “Whoa’p! Whoa’p!” he called repeatedly, as with loud snorts the imaginary steed reared and plunged about; finally the excited animal came to a standstill. Looking at Warren, the boy said, “Gid told me to come and tell you, he will meet you down below the barn, at the east gate, right after school this afternoon. He told me to tell you again he can lick you good.”

After some prancing about, the boy ran off, clapping his hips with his hands to imitate the sound of galloping hoofs.

Gideon had accepted Warren’s challenge, and we had no misgivings as to the outcome, for we had every confidence in Warren’s courage and strength. What concerned us most was Jim’s meddling with us and the means by which we could prevent his farther interference with our peace. He had made trouble with other gangs just in this way. We were still discussing this matter when the school-bell rang, and we went to the house together.

The boys who had already taken their seats looked up at us as we entered the schoolroom, then they turned their glances upon Gideon to see how he would behave. The two boys, Gideon and Warren, stared at each other defiantly; the rest saw there was no courage lacking in either, and they expected a lively battle between the two. Jim pretended to be studying; but we knew that he was closely watching the victims of his machinations to see how they would act. Jim never studied; he was always at the foot of his class, and boys younger than he were far in advance of him.

===Chinese===
“记住了！”莱斯特望着他弟弟叮嘱。“吉德要是使那手抱腰托下巴，你就照弗兰克教你的破法办。”

我们正说着，听见赤脚拍打硬地的声响，很快一个男孩来到我们面前，学着烈马的做派。“吁！吁！”他一遍遍吆喝，那匹想象的坐骑响亮地打着响鼻，扬蹄奋鬃；末了这匹兴奋的牲口站定了。男孩望着沃伦说：“吉德叫我来告诉你，今天下午一放学，他在牲口棚下面、东门那儿等你。他还叫我再捎一句，他要好好揍扁你。”

那男孩又撒欢刨了几下蹄子，跑开了，双手拍着屁股学马蹄疾驰的声响。

基甸应了沃伦的挑战，我们对结果毫无疑虑，因为对沃伦的胆气和力气我们信得过。最叫我们挂心的是吉姆来搅和，以及怎么挡住他再来搅我们的清净。他就是用这一手跟别的帮派结的怨。我们正议着，上课铃响了，于是一起回楼里去。

已落座的男孩们见我们进教室，都抬头看，随即把目光转到基甸身上，看他会怎样。两个男孩，基甸和沃伦，挑衅地对瞪；大家看出两人都不缺胆量，都等着看一场好斗。吉姆装作用功；我们知道他在盯他那套阴谋的牺牲品，看他们如何举动。吉姆从不用功；永远是他班里的末名，比他小的男孩都远远超过他。

===Original===
At last the monotonous recitations came to an end. We sang a song about “Pretty little zephyrs,” then Graybeard closed the school with the usual religious exercises.

The boys gathered in groups and walked down to the place designated for the combat. We followed slowly, as we wanted time to give all the instructions necessary to Warren. A large ring had been formed by the boys, and Gid was already in the centre with his coat off and his sleeves rolled up. Jim glanced at us as though impatient for our coming. As we neared the ring, someone said, in a voice loud enough for us to hear, “They’re not coming very fast. I guess they’re ’fraid!”

Brush stepped hastily forward and asked, “Who said we’re afraid? Whoever said it, let him come out here and I’ll show him whether we’re afraid or not!”

No one answered. There were few boys in the school who would without fear accept a challenge from Brush.

===Chinese===
单调的背诵总算到了头。我们唱了一支“漂亮的小和风”，然后灰胡子照例做了礼拜，放了学。

男孩们三五成群，往约定的斗场走去。我们慢慢跟在后面，想争取时间把要紧的嘱咐都交代给沃伦。男孩们已围成一个大圈，吉德早站在中央，脱了外衣，卷起袖子。吉姆扫了我们一眼，像嫌我们来得慢。我们走近圈子，有人开了腔，嗓门刚好让我们听见：“他们走得可真慢。我看是怕了！”

布拉什抢步上前，问：“谁说我们怕了？谁说的站出来，我让他瞧瞧我们怕不怕！”

没人应声。学校里敢应布拉什挑战的男孩没几个。

===Original===
A place was cleared for us, and Warren, after handing me his coat, entered the ring. The two boys approached each other and stopped within a few feet.

“Did you tell Jim you could lick me?” asked Warren, looking his opponent square in the eye.

“Yes. And I can do it too,” was the bold reply.

“You can’t do it!” exclaimed Warren, striking Gideon in the chest.

===Chinese===
给我们让出了一块空地；沃伦把外衣交给我，进了圈。两个男孩彼此逼近，在几步之内站定。

“你跟吉姆说过你能揍扁我？”沃伦问，眼睛直直瞪住对手。

“说过。而且我真能。”回答得毫不示弱。

“你揍不了！”沃伦喊，一拳打在基甸胸口。

===Original===
Then followed an exciting scene. Gideon rushed at Warren, and aimed blow after blow at his face, but our boy skilfully parried each attack. Round and round within the ring the two boys carried on their strife, neither one prevailing. For a while no serious blows were dealt, finally, in an unguarded moment Warren received a hard thrust in the left side which made him gasp; whereat Gid’s gang shouted in chorus, “*Choo-ie!*”6 After this success Gideon grew reckless and struck wildly, and Warren was a little too anxious to put in a good hit before the proper moment. Gid made another effort at his antagonist’s ribs, but the blow fell short; then Warren made a lunge at Gid’s face; he dodged, but not quickly enough to save his ear from a bad scraping from Warren’s knuckles. “*Choo-ie!*” cried Lester and the rest of us at this success; but Gid’s next movement threw us into dismay, he had suddenly seized Warren around the waist while his arms were uplifted. Gid put his chin against Warren’s chest and began pulling in his back. Warren tried to twist Gid’s neck; but there was no use in that, Warren was slowly giving way. If he should fall the battle would be won by Gideon.

“Put your arms under his and push!” I said to Warren in an undertone. I couldn’t help doing it.

Isaac, a blustering little chap and one of Gid’s gang overheard me; stepping forward and pointing his finger at me, he angrily exclaimed, “Frank, you know that ain’t fair, we don’t do that way.”

“You do worse than that,” I retorted. “The whole four of you jumped on me in the schoolroom; that wasn’t fair, but I licked you! Wait till Warren and Gid get through, then I’ll see you!”

Warren had heard my words, and acted on them at once, and so released himself from Gideon’s dangerous grasp. Then they went to sparring again. In making a thrust Warren stumbled on a round stone and fell on one knee, before he could rise Gid put in a blow that cut Warren’s under lip. “*Choo-ie!*” exclaimed the friends of Gid. It seemed for a moment as though the victory would be against us. The struggle now became desperate. Gid was blowing hard, but there was still considerable reserve of strength in Warren. Gid repeatedly tried to grasp his antagonist’s waist, but was every time cleverly brought about again to fists.

===Chinese===
接下来是一场好戏。基甸扑向沃伦，一拳一拳照他脸上招呼，可我们这男孩灵巧地挡开了每一击。两个男孩在圈子里转来转去地厮打，谁也占不到上风。有一阵谁都没挨着要紧的拳头，末了，一个疏忽，沃伦左肋重重挨了一记，打得他直抽气；吉德那伙人齐声欢呼：“Choo-ie！”6 得了这一手，基甸胆子放大，抡得没了章法；沃伦又太急着没到火候就还上一记好拳。吉德又朝对手肋部来了一下，拳够不着；沃伦直取吉德的脸；他一闪，却慢了半拍，耳朵上结结实实蹭了沃伦一记指节。“Choo-ie！”莱斯特和我们大伙儿为这一手叫好；可吉德下一招让我们心里一沉——他趁沃伦双臂扬起，忽然拦腰把他抱住了。吉德下巴抵住沃伦胸口，往回坐劲。沃伦想拧他的脖子；可不顶用，沃伦在慢慢吃不住。他要是一倒，这一仗就是基甸赢了。

“胳膊从他胳膊底下穿上去，往外撑！”我低声对沃伦说。我忍不住。

艾萨克（Isaac），一个咋咋呼呼的小家伙、吉德一伙的，偷听了我的话；他跨上一步，指头点着我说：“弗兰克，你知道这不公平，我们不兴这样。”

“你们干的比这过分，”我回敬。“你们四个在教室里一齐扑过我；那公平吗？可我把你们揍了！等沃伦和吉德分出胜负，再轮到收拾你！”

沃伦听见了我的话，立刻照办，从基甸那危险的搂抱里挣了出来。两人又拉开了拳。沃伦一个冲刺，绊在一块圆石上，单膝跪倒；没等他起身，吉德一拳捣破了他的下唇。“Choo-ie！”吉德的伙伴们欢呼。一时间胜利像要倒向他们那边。争斗白热化了。吉德喘得厉害，沃伦却还有的是后劲。吉德一再想抱对手的腰，可每回都被人巧妙地逼回拳来。

===Original===
Warren’s shirt front was bloody and his short hair stood straight up, giving him a frightful aspect. Gid’s thrusts and parries now grew visibly weaker, but he showed no signs of yielding. He lowered his fists to give an under cut, thus leaving his face unguarded, quick as a flash Warren’s right arm shot out, and with a sickening thud his fist landed square on Gid’s nose. The blood spurted; the boy was stunned, and, before he could recover, he received another blow on the eye.

The fight was ended, and Gid’s friends dragged him away more dead than alive.

Warren came to us smiling as widely as his swollen lip would permit.

“You did first rate, old boy!” said Brush, slapping Warren’s back.

“He’ll never want to fight you again,” added Lester.

===Chinese===
沃伦的前襟血迹斑斑，短发根根直立，模样骇人。吉德的直拳和格挡眼见弱了下来，却没有一点认输的意思。他垂下双拳想使一记下勾，脸上便空了；说时迟那时快，沃伦右臂直捣出去，拳头不偏不倚砸在吉德的鼻子上，闷响叫人牙酸。血迸了出来；那男孩懵了，还没缓过神，眼上又挨了一拳。

这一仗打完了，吉德的朋友们把他拖走，他只剩了半口气。

沃伦朝我们走来，肿着的嘴唇能咧多开就咧多开地笑着。

“打得漂亮极了，老伙计！”布拉什拍着沃伦的背说。

“他再也不想跟你打了。”莱斯特补了一句。

===Original===
I helped Warren to put on his coat, then I looked around to see where Edwin was. I saw him standing before Jim, who was watching us with his wicked grin. They both spoke, but I could not hear them for the noise of the talk around me. Suddenly Edwin’s long arm darted out, his fist came square on Jim’s cheek with a resounding whack. Jim’s face became livid, and the spot upon which the blow fell twitched convulsively. When the natural color returned to his face, Jim deliberately pulled off his coat; he was going to fight Edwin. It was an uneven match; Jim stood a head taller and was heavier than Edwin.

“What’s the matter?” asked Brush, as he came up; “what are you going to do?”

“We’re going to fight,” replied Edwin; “I hit him because he made that trouble.”

“Jim,” said Brush, stepping forward and rolling up his sleeves, “I don’t think it would be unfair for two of us to fight you. You are bigger than any of us, so I am going to help Edwin to thrash you. You’ve been making mischief for others, now it’s going to come to you.”

The boys gathered around the three to see another fight, but were disappointed. Jim made no further demonstration, but stood looking at the two boys; at last he muttered something to himself, and, picking up his coat, pushed his way out of the crowd.

All the boys pointed their fingers at Jim, and shouted, “Ah, coward!” Jim turned his head and looked at them sulkily, but went on, and no one cared to follow him.

===Chinese===
我帮沃伦穿上外衣，然后环顾埃德温去哪儿了。我看见他站在吉姆面前——吉姆正带着他那副歹毒的笑望着我们。两人都说了句什么，可周围人声嘈杂，我听不清。忽然埃德温的长臂闪出，拳头端端正正砸在吉姆腮上，脆响一声。吉姆的脸变得铁青，挨拳的那块肉直抽。等脸上血色回来，吉姆不慌不忙脱下外衣；他要跟埃德温打。这是一场不对等的较量：吉姆比埃德温高出一头，分量也重。

“怎么回事？”布拉什走过来问；“你们要干什么？”

“我们要打一架，”埃德温答；“我打他，因为祸是他惹的。”

“吉姆，”布拉什卷起袖子上前说，“我们两个打你一个，我看也不算欺负你。你比我们哪个都大，所以我打算帮埃德温揍你。你净给别人使坏，如今轮到你自己头上了。”

男孩们围住他们仨等着看第二场架，却落了空。吉姆没有再摆架势，只站着望那两个男孩；末了他自言自语咕哝了句什么，拾起外衣，从人堆里挤出去。

所有男孩都指着吉姆喊：“啊，胆小鬼！”吉姆回头阴沉沉地瞪了他们一眼，还是走了，没人屑于追他。

## IX William T. Sherman / 威廉·T·谢尔曼

===Original===
He stood on the third board of the fence from the ground, and leaned with his elbows on the top one, now and again kicking with his moccasined foot a loose panel. How long he had been standing there rattling that loose board no one knew, but in time one of the boys noticed him, and suddenly he became an object of the greatest interest among the boys of all sizes at the school. Boys who were playing down by the river, up by the spring, and over by the saw mill came running to see the stranger; and how the word reached them was as much of a mystery as the appearance of the little figure on the fence.

Everyone was eager to pelt him with a question, and get as close to him as possible. He answered the questions in monosyllables; but he showed objection to any near approach, by freeing his bare arms from his little buffalo robe and pointing a wooden popgun at the eye of the boy who was inclined to be too familiar. We kept at him until we found out that his name was Thinʻ-je-zhin-ga, which, translated into the language of the Missionaries, signified Little Tail.7

He had come over from the village to see the school, and was as much interested in us as we were in him. All at once something attracted his attention; his black eyes sparkled, out came one arm from under his robe, and he pointed with a very dirty little finger and said, “Give me one of those!”

The coveted object was a brass button on the jacket of one of the small students. When Little Tail was asked what he wanted to do with it, he said, “Tie it to my scalp-lock.” This sounded very funny to us, and we all laughed. The little chap retreated into his robe, covered his head, and looked out at us with one eye.

===Chinese===
他站在栅栏离地第三块板上，胳膊肘搭在顶板上，不时用鹿皮鞋踢一脚那块松了的栏板。他在那儿把那块松板摇响了多久，没人知道；反正有个男孩终于注意到了他，转眼间他成了全校大大小小男孩最关注的对象。在河边玩的、泉边玩的、锯木厂那边玩的男孩都跑来看这生人；消息怎么传到他们那儿的，同栅栏上这个小身影的出现一样是个谜。

人人都想拿问题砸他，都想离他越近越好。他单音节地答着；可谁想凑近，他就从野牛皮小袍里抽出光胳膊，把一杆木头啵啵枪对准那过于亲热的男孩的眼睛。我们缠了他半天，问出他名叫 Thinʻ-je-zhin-ga——译成传教士的语言，意思是“小尾巴”（Little Tail）。7

他是从村里过来看学校的，我们对他的兴趣，抵得上他对我们的。忽然有什么东西吸引了他；黑眼睛一亮，一条胳膊从袍子底下伸出来，用一根脏兮兮的小指头指着说：“给我一个那个！”

那眼馋的东西，是一个小学生外衣上的黄铜纽扣。有人问小尾巴要它做什么，他说：“拴在我的头皮辫上。”这话在我们听来太逗了，哄堂大笑。小家伙缩回袍子里，蒙住头，露出一只眼睛看我们。

===Original===
The bell rang for dinner; and there followed a general scramble to appear promptly at the table, and no thought was given to the queer little visitor. Being the last boy to enter the house, I turned to look back at him, and there he stood perched upon the fence, staring after us as though he wondered why he was so suddenly deserted.

When we came out from dinner, he was still on the fence, but he was busy. He had an ear of roasted corn and was shelling the kernels; when he had nearly a handful he tipped his head back, poured the grains into his mouth, and ate them with relish. After he had stripped half of the cob, he seemed to be satisfied, and the remainder disappeared in the recesses of his robe. As he finished his corn dinner, one of the schoolboys said to him, “Little Tail, how would you like to stay and live with us here?”

“I would like it,” he promptly replied.

“Will you stay?”

“Yes.”

It was soon reported to the superintendent that a new pupil had come. When the afternoon session opened and the pupils were seated, Little Tail was given a seat at one of the desks, but to our delight he slid down and sat on the floor. The teacher rapped the top of his desk with a ruler and cried, “Silence!” and order was restored.

===Chinese===
开饭铃响了；接着是一阵赶着上桌的大乱，谁也顾不上那古怪的小客人。我是最后一个进屋的，回头看他；他还高踞在栅栏上，直勾勾望着我们，像是不明白自己怎么突然被撇下了。

我们吃完饭出来，他还在栅栏上，可忙活着呢。他手里一穗烤玉米，正剥粒儿；剥到快一把，就把头一仰，把粒儿倒进嘴里，吃得津津有味。啃完半穗，他似乎满足了，剩下的就收进袍子深处。他的玉米饭一吃完，一个学生就问他：“小尾巴，留下来跟我们一块儿住，好不好？”

“好。”他答得干脆。

“那你留下？”

“留。”

很快就有人报给校监，说来了个新学生。下午开课、学生们坐定后，给小尾巴在一张课桌前安了座位；可他出溜下去坐在了地板上，惹得我们好乐。老师拿戒尺敲了敲桌顶，喊：“肃静！”秩序恢复了。

===Original===
“What is the name of the new boy?” he asked.

“Thinʻ-je-zhin-ga,” answered one of the boys.

Graybeard tried to repeat the name, but only set the whole school laughing. While this was going on, Little Tail reached down to his belt and drew out a roll of milkweed fibre. It was his ammunition. He tore off enough to make a bullet, chewed it, and, bringing the breach of the popgun to his mouth, inserted the ball, twisting the gun with his hands while he pressed the wad in with his teeth, making many motions with his head. By pounding the butt of the rammer on the floor, he drove the ball to the firing point; then raising the gun he began forcing the ball with vigorous thrusts, aiming it at a mischievous boy who sat opposite making faces at him. Bang! went the weapon; the bullet, instead of hitting the object aimed at, struck Graybeard in the face, and made him throw his head back. We covered our faces to suppress the giggles that bubbled up at this mishap. The wounded man looked sharply at the young artillerist, who, seeing the mischief he had done, very slyly thrust his gun into his robe, and, keeping an eye on his victim, sat perfectly still.

The teacher looked serious, then we became scared. After a moment his face relaxed, and he said in a pleasant tone, “We must have the name of the new boy on the Register, but we cannot have any name that is unpronounceable. We shall have to give him an English name. Will you suggest one?”

A number of hands went up and as many historic names were offered and rejected. Finally it was determined to call him William T. Sherman and that name was entered upon the Register.

===Chinese===
“新来的男孩叫什么名字？”他问。

“Thinʻ-je-zhin-ga。”一个男孩答。

灰胡子试着念这名字，只逗得全校大笑。乱着这当口，小尾巴把手伸到腰带底下，抽出一卷马利筋纤维——那是他的弹药。他撕下够做一颗弹丸的一块，嚼了嚼，把枪尾凑到嘴边塞进弹丸，两手拧着枪，牙咬着往里压填弹塞，脑袋做出种种姿势。他把通条屁股往地上顿，把弹丸送到击发位；然后端起枪，一下一下使劲捅着送弹，瞄着一个坐在对面朝他做鬼脸的捣蛋男孩。砰！武器响了；弹丸没打中瞄准的目标，打在了灰胡子脸上，打得他头向后一仰。我们捂住脸，强压着这场祸事激起的一串咯咯笑。受伤的人狠狠瞪着这位少年炮手；小家伙一看闯了祸，贼溜溜地把枪捅回袍子里，眼睛盯住受害者，坐得纹丝不动。

老师神色一沉，我们跟着害怕起来。过了一会儿他的脸缓和下来，用愉快的声调说：“学籍簿上得有新同学的名字，可没法登记一个念不出来的名字。我们得给他起个英文名。你们提议一个吧。”

好几只手举了起来，好几历史名人的名字报上来又被否掉。最后议定叫他威廉·T·谢尔曼（William T. Sherman），这名字登上了学籍簿。

===Original===
After school a few boys were detailed to wash and dress the new arrival; so, with arms full of clothing, towels, and other bathing appliances, the lad was taken up to the boys’ dormitory. The first thing to be done was to cut his long hair. A towel was put around his neck, and soon the shears were singing a tune about his ears. He seemed to enjoy it, and laughed at the jokes made by the boys; but when by some chance he caught sight of his scalp-lock lying on the floor like a little black snake, he put his fists into his eyes and fell to sobbing as though his heart would break.

“Pshaw!” said little Isaac, rubbing his closely cropped head, “mine was longer than yours when it was cut off, but I didn’t cry!”

“Mine too!” exclaimed Abraham, picking up the braided look and putting it where his had been; at which the rest of the boys laughed.

When the bath was over, William T. Sherman was dressed. He was delighted with his brand-new clothes, particularly with the long row of brass buttons that adorned the front of the jacket. When it came to the shoes, his grief for the lost scalp-lock was clean forgotten, and he strutted about to show the boys that his shining black shoes sang to his satisfaction.

William T. Sherman was quick to learn, and by the time winter was over he was speaking the peculiar English used by the boys of the school; he said, “fool bird,” for quail; “first time,” for long ago, and other Indian expressions turned into English. He was fond of arithmetic, and spent much time ciphering on his slate; he would write down the figure 1, 2, or 3, add to it a string of aughts, and then try to read them off. Grammar he abhorred, and in the spelling class, he held a permanent place at the foot. In outdoor sports he excelled; he could beat any boy of his size in leaping and running, and we had yet to learn other things in which he was expert.

===Chinese===
放学后，派了几个男孩给新来的洗身换装；于是小伙子被带上男生宿舍，大家怀里抱满了衣裳、毛巾和别的洗浴家什。头一件事是剪掉他的长发。一条毛巾围上脖子，剪刀很快在他耳朵周围唱起歌来。他好像挺受用，男孩们的玩笑他也跟着笑；可无意间瞥见自己的头皮辫像条小黑蛇躺在地上，他登时把拳头塞进眼睛，抽抽噎噎哭得心都要碎了。

“呸！”小艾萨克摩着自家剪得精光的脑袋说，“剪的时候我的比你的长呢，我可没哭！”

“我也没哭！”亚伯拉罕喊，拾起那条辫好的发辫，摆到自己的后脑勺上；其余男孩全笑了。

洗完澡，威廉·T·谢尔曼穿戴一新。他对崭新的衣裳欢喜透了，尤其外衣前襟那一长排黄铜纽扣。轮到鞋子，他把丧头皮辫的悲痛忘了个干净，大摇大摆地走来走去给男孩们看，他那乌黑锃亮的鞋吱吱作响，唱得他心满意足。

威廉·T·谢尔曼学得快，一冬天过完，他就说起学校男孩们那套特别的英语来：说“傻瓜鸟”指鹌鹑；说“头一回”指很久以前，还有别的从印第安话翻过来的英语。他爱算术，成天在石板上演算；他会写下数字 1、2 或 3，后头拖一串零，然后试着把它们念出来。语法他深恶痛绝；拼读班里的末名他占了个永久席位。户外的运动他样样出众；跑跳方面，同个头的男孩没一个赢得了他；而他在别处的本事，我们还要慢慢领教。

===Original===
One day, during the great June rise, all the boys were at the river watching the huge drift logs floating down the muddy Missouri.

“Say, boys!” exclaimed Ulysses S. Grant, thrusting his hands deep into his pockets; “I bet one hundred dollars that river is strong. I wouldn’t like to swim in it; I’m sure the eddies would pull me under.”

Gideon, who was always boasting of what his father could do, shouted, “My father could swim clear acrost and back again; he ain’t ’fraid of eddies. He﻿—”

“What’s that?” cried a number of boys, startled by a heavy splash in the water. We all watched, and two brown feet came to the surface, wiggled, and disappeared. After a moment a round black head slowly arose. “Ha! Ha! I’m not ’fraid eddy!” shouted William T. Sherman, for it was he. A few vigorous strokes brought him to shore again.

“Take off your shirts and pants, boys, let’s swim,” he said.

===Chinese===
一天，正逢六月大涨水，男孩们都聚在河边，看巨大的浮木顺浑浊的密苏里河漂下来。

“哎，伙计们！”尤利西斯·S·格兰特把双手深深插进兜里喊；“我赌一百块钱，这河劲头大得很。我可不想在这儿游；漩涡准得把我拽下去。”

基甸——那位总吹嘘自己父亲如何如何的——喊道：“我爹能一口气游到对岸，再游回来；他才不怕漩涡。他——”

“那是什么？”好几个男孩叫起来，被水里一声重重的溅响吓了一跳。大家盯住水面，两只棕色脚掌浮了上来，扑腾几下，又没了。片刻，一个圆圆的黑脑袋慢慢冒出来。“哈！哈！我才不怕漩涡！”威廉·T·谢尔曼喊——正是他。几下有力的划水，他又上了岸。

“把衬衫裤子脱了，伙计们，游起来。”他说。

===Original===
We did so, and timidly splashed about the shallow edges of the water. A large tree was drifting down near the middle of the river. William ran up along the bank for quite a distance, and then plunged into the water. It was a beautiful sight to watch him as he threw his arms up and down, moving swiftly toward the tree; he reached it, dived under it, and came up on the other side; then he scrambled on the trunk and shouted for us to come, but none of us dared to go. After a moment he stood up on the tree, flourished an imaginary whip, and cried, “Git up, there!” with a succession of swear words﻿—genuine swear words. He was imitating the Agency teamster, and did not know what he was saying. He had heard the servant of the Government urge on his horses by such terms, and he was merely repeating them. Those of us who had been at the Mission a long time, and had all the Shorter Catechism in our little heads, and were orthodox by compulsion, if not by conviction, were horrified to hear those dreadful words uttered by a pupil of our school; for we knew some severe punishment awaited the little sinner should there be a traitor among us to make it known to Graybeard.

Before we had fairly recovered from our shock at hearing this swearing, we were startled by a cry, “Job is drowning!” Not one of us moved, we were so frightened; but, quick as a flash, William T. Sherman sprang from his imaginary wagon, swam swiftly to the boy, caught him by the hair as he was going down for the last time, and brought him to the surface. “Kick! Kick!” he shouted; “make your arms go! Don’t stop!” And after a hard struggle the two boys landed.

Job had swallowed considerable water, and become very sick. We didn’t know what to do for him; but after we had rubbed and pommelled him, and held him by the heels head downward, he felt better; then we took him to the Mission and put him to bed.

On our way back Sherman spoke very little, but those of us who had been frightened into helplessness had much to say as to what we did or might have done to save Job.

===Chinese===
我们脱了，怯生生地在浅水里扑腾。一棵大树正从河当中漂下来。威廉沿着河岸跑上去老远，一头扎进水里。看他双臂上下翻飞、飞快朝那棵树游去，真是一幅好看的景象；他赶上了树，从底下潜过，从那边冒出头；然后爬上树干，喊我们过去，可谁也不敢。过了一会儿他站上树身，挥着一条想象中的鞭子，连声吆喝：“驾，走！”还夹着一串骂人的话——地地道道的骂人话。他在学代理处的赶车把式，自己并不知道在说什么。他听政府的雇工就用这些词儿吆喝牲口，照着学舌罢了。我们这些在传教所待久了、小脑袋里装满了《小要理问答》、就算不是出自真心也算得上被逼成的正教徒，听见本校学生嘴里吐出这些可怕的字眼，个个吓坏了；因为我们知道，只要我们中间出个叛徒，把这事捅给灰胡子，小罪人等着的就是一顿重罚。

我们还没从听见骂人的震惊里缓过来，又被一声喊吓着了：“约伯（Job）淹着了！”谁都没动，全吓瘫了；可说时迟那时快，威廉·T·谢尔曼从他想象的大车上纵身一跳，飞快游到那男孩跟前，趁他最后一次往下沉，一把揪住头发，把他带出水面。“踢！踢！”他喊；“胳膊划！别停！”一番苦斗之后，两个男孩上了岸。

约伯灌了一肚子水，难受得很。我们不知怎么救他；揉了半天、捶了半天，又倒提脚跟控了控，他才好些；然后我们把他送回传教所，安置上床。

回去的路上谢尔曼话很少；倒是我们这些吓软了手脚的，对救约伯时自己干了什么、本来还能干什么，说个没完。

===Original===
At supper Graybeard as usual counted the boys, and found one missing, “Where’s Job?” he asked.

“He’s got the th’tomick ache,” said Daniel, his mouth full and his spoon raised halfway with a new supply.

School went on the next morning as though nothing had happened. The teachers had not heard of the drowning and the rescue; but the girls had learned all about it and threw admiring glances at Sherman: to them he had become a hero, and each of the different gangs among the boys now wanted this hero as a member.

The recitations for the afternoon session were over, and the bell was tapped as a signal to put away our books and slates, and struck again to call us to order. When all arms were folded, there followed an ominous silence. Graybeard slowly looked around the schoolroom, as though to read every face turned up to him, then he spoke:

“I have been told that some of the boys in this school are in the habit of swearing; that is one of the things you are forbidden to do. It is wicked to swear, and any boy that I find has been doing so I shall punish very severely. I want you to remember this. After the closing exercises William T. Sherman will come to my room; I have something to say to him.”

All eyes on the boys’ side turned toward William as we chanted the Lord’s prayer; then Graybeard made his usual supplication, during which the big girls twisted their necks to look at their hero.

===Chinese===
晚饭时灰胡子照例点人数，发现少了一个。“约伯呢？”他问。

“他肚子疼，”但以理（Daniel）说，嘴里塞得满满的，勺子举到半截又添了新的。

第二天早晨照常上课，像什么都没发生过。老师们没听说溺水获救的事；女孩们却全知道了，一齐朝谢尔曼投去爱慕的目光：在她们眼里他成了英雄，男孩们的各帮各派如今都想要这位英雄入伙。

下午的背诵结束了；轻敲一记铃，是收书本石板的号令，再敲一记，叫我们归位坐好。人人抱臂当胸，接着是一阵不祥的寂静。灰胡子缓缓环视教室，像要读出仰望着他的每一张脸，然后开口：

“有人告诉我，本校有些男孩有骂人的习惯；那是明令禁止的事情之一。骂人是罪，哪个男孩叫我查出骂了人，我要重重地罚。你们要记住这话。散学礼拜之后，威廉·T·谢尔曼到我房间来；我有话对他说。”

我们齐诵主祷文时，男孩这边的目光齐刷刷投向威廉；随后灰胡子照例祷告，大姑娘们拧着脖子瞧她们的英雄。

===Original===
The exit from the schoolroom was quite orderly, but as soon as the groups of boys passed into the hall, they set up a shouting and singing, and made off to their different resorts for play. We, the Middle Five, were the last to go; and, as had been hastily arranged between us, I went to Graybeard and asked some trivial question in order to give time for Brush to go and advise Sherman as to what answers to make if he was asked as to his being guilty of swearing.

“When he asks you if you been swearing, say, ‘No, sir, I don’t know what swear is,’ ” said Brush to Sherman.

“All right.”

“Then tell him you been saying what you heard Agency man say to horses; but you don’t know what those words mean, maybe they’re swear words, you don’t know.”

Graybeard went up to his room, followed by William T. Sherman, who for the first time entered that apartment. Boys who committed serious offences were disciplined in that place. I was taken there for fighting Andrew Johnson; Brush took his punishment there when he nearly cut Jonathan’s ear off with a wooden sword. Most of us had had peculiar experiences in that room.

===Chinese===
出教室倒还秩序井然，可男孩们一组一组刚拐进门厅就喊的喊、唱的唱，散到各自的玩乐场去了。我们中间五人走在最后；照我们匆忙合计好的，我去问灰胡子一个无关紧要的问题，好拖住时间，让布拉什去教谢尔曼：问到骂人一事该怎么答。

“他要是问你骂没骂人，你就说：‘没有，先生，我不知道什么叫骂人。’”布拉什对谢尔曼说。

“好。”

“然后告诉他，你说的都是听代理处的人吆喝马说的话；可那些词什么意思你不知道，兴许是骂人的话，你不知道。”

灰胡子上楼回房，威廉·T·谢尔曼跟在后头，头一回进那间屋子。犯了大过的男孩都在那里受训。我因为同安德鲁·约翰逊打架被带去过；布拉什用木头剑差点削掉约拿单的耳朵，也是在那里领的罚。我们大多数人都在那间屋子里有过特别的经历。

===Original===
William T. Sherman had come to us direct from a tent; our bare schoolroom and playroom were all that he had seen of the furnishings of a civilized dwelling, so when he was suddenly ushered into Graybeard’s room he was quite dazzled by the bright draperies, pictures, and the polished furniture. He stood with hands in his pockets, mouth and eyes wide-open staring at the things, although twice requested by his host to sit down.

William timidly took the chair assigned him. It rocked backwards, and up went his feet; he clutched wildly at the arms, and the chair rocked forward; he got his footing, then sat perfectly still, fearing the chair would fall over with him.

Graybeard took a seat facing the boy, and began to question him, “I was told that you had been swearing; is it true?”

Bewilderment at new sights, and the flight of the rocking-chair had put Brush’s promptings out of Sherman’s head, and in his confusion he answered, “Yes, sir﻿—ma’am.”

===Chinese===
威廉·T·谢尔曼是直接从帐篷里来到我们中间的；我们那光秃秃的教室和游戏室，就是他对文明居所陈设的全部见识；因此冷不丁被领进灰胡子的房间，明亮的帘幔、图画和上光的家具照得他眼花缭乱。他双手插兜站着，嘴和眼睛张得老大，盯着这些东西，主人两次请他坐，他都没听见似的。

威廉怯生生地坐进指给他的椅子。椅子往后一悠，他双脚朝天；他慌忙乱抓扶手，椅子又往前一悠；他找到了着力处，随后坐得笔直，生怕椅子连他一起翻倒。

灰胡子在男孩对面坐下，开始问话：“我听说你骂人了；有没有这回事？”

新鲜景象的眩晕，加上摇椅的翻腾，早把布拉什教的词儿从谢尔曼脑袋里摇出去了；他慌乱之下答道：“有，先生——夫人。”

===Original===
“It is wicked to swear, and you must be taught to know that it is. Now say what I say,” and Graybeard repeated the third commandment, until Sherman could say it without assistance, and then bade him to keep on until told to stop.

Poor William sat in the treacherous rocking-chair repeating this commandment, while Graybeard wrote at his desk. William might as well have sat there imitating the cry of some animal or bird, for his mind was not dwelling upon the words he was uttering, but following his eyes as they moved from one strange object to another﻿—the pictures, the gilt frames, the seashells, the clock on the mantelpiece, then something hanging near the window absorbed his attention, and his tongue and lips ceased to move as he drew with his finger on his knee the figure 1, adding to it a number of aughts. Graybeard noted the pause, and said, “Go on, William, don’t stop.” After some little prompting, the boy resumed, but his finger kept moving, making the figure 1 and a string of aughts after it.

When Graybeard and William T. Sherman left the schoolroom, Brush and I and the rest of the five went toward the spring and sat under the large elm. Brush lay down on the grass and read a book he had borrowed from the superintendent, while the rest of us talked.

“I’d like to see that boy who told on William T. Sherman; I’d give him a licking,” said Warren.

“I’d kick him hard,” added Edwin.

===Chinese===
“骂人是罪，得教你明白它是罪。现在跟着我说，”灰胡子把第三诫领着他念了一遍又一遍，直到谢尔曼能不等提词自己念下来，然后吩咐他一直念下去，叫停再停。

可怜的威廉坐在那把心怀叵测的摇椅里，一遍遍背着诫命，灰胡子在桌前写字。威廉坐在那儿，还不如学什么鸟兽叫呢，因为他的心思根本不在嘴里的字上，而是跟着眼睛从一个陌生物件游到另一个——那些画、涂金的框子、海贝、壁炉架上的钟，随后挂在窗边的一件什么吸住了他，他的舌头嘴唇不动了，手指在膝盖上画起数字 1，又添上一串零。灰胡子察觉停顿，说：“接着念，威廉，别停。”提了几次词，男孩才续上，可手指还在动，画着 1 和后头拖长的一串零。

灰胡子和威廉·T·谢尔曼离开教室后，布拉什、我，还有五人中的其余几个，走向泉水，在那棵大榆树下坐了。布拉什躺在草地上，读一本从校监那里借来的书，我们其余人聊天。

“我倒想见见告威廉·T·谢尔曼状的那个家伙；我要好好揍他一顿。”沃伦说。

“我要狠狠踹他。”埃德温加一句。

===Original===
“I bet it’s that telltale Edson; he ought to be thumped!” I suggested.

While we were talking, William came and sat down with us. Every now and then a quivering sigh would escape him, although he tried not to show that he had been crying. Little Bob, believing as we did that William had been whipped, and, desiring to express sympathy, said, “Say, did it hurt?” William did not answer; nobody ever answered Bob.

“What did Graybeard do to you?” I asked, turning to William.

“He made me sit down and say a commandment one hundred times.”

“Which one was it? Say it to us.”

“I don’t want to say it; I said it enough.” After a pause he asked, “What is swear?”

===Chinese===
“我打赌准是那个长舌的埃德森（Edson）；该捶他一顿！”我猜。

我们正说着，威廉来了，挨着我们坐下。他不时从胸腔里漏出一声颤抖的叹息，尽管他竭力不叫人看出哭过。小鲍勃和我们一样，认定威廉挨了鞭子，想表示同情，便问：“哎，疼不疼？”威廉不答；谁也从来不答鲍勃的话。

“灰胡子把你怎么样了？”我转向威廉问。

“他叫我坐下，把一条诫命念一百遍。”

“哪一条？念给我们听听。”

“我不想念；我念够了。”顿了顿，他问：“什么叫骂人？”

===Original===
“When you call God names, that’s swear,” said Warren.

“I don’t do that. I know God, it’s the same Omahas call Wa-kon-da; but I don’t know what means lord.”

“It’s a man just like big chief,” explained Lester; “he has plenty of horses and lots of money. When he tells anybody to do anything, he got to do it; that’s a lord.”

“Is Graybeard lord?”

“No, Graybeard isn’t lord.”

“Say, boys, a one and six aughts is one million, ain’t it?”

“Yes,” we answered in chorus.

===Chinese===
“你骂上帝的名儿，那就是骂人。”沃伦说。

“我不干那个。我认得上帝，就是奥马哈人叫 Wa-kon-da 的那位；可我不知道‘主’是什么意思。”

“主嘛，就是个跟大酋长一样的人，”莱斯特解释；“他有的是马，有的是钱。他叫谁干什么，谁就得干；那就是主。”

“灰胡子是主吗？”

“不，灰胡子不是主。”

“哎，伙计们，一个一后头六个零是一百万，对不对？”

“对。”我们齐声答。

===Original===
“Graybeard is lord. He’s got one million dollars. I saw it on a book hanging by his window; it had a name, I can’t say it, then Bank and Cap’tal, and then a one and six aughts﻿—that’s a million. He’s got one million dollars!”

Brush threw his book down, raised himself on his elbow and looked at us with a smile; then he said, “I know that book William T. Sherman saw, it’s the book Graybeard counts the days by, and it’s got on it what they call advertisement. That bank wants people to know it has one million dollars capital to go by; I learned that in my arithmetic. Graybeard isn’t a lord; he’s a missionary﻿—the same kind that goes to Africa and Greenland’s icy mountains.”

===Chinese===
“灰胡子是主。他有一百万块钱。我在他窗边挂的一本书上看见的；上头有个名字，我念不来，后头是银行和资本，再后头就是一个一和六个零——那是一百万。他有一百万块钱！”

布拉什把书一撂，支起胳膊肘，笑着看我们；然后说：“我知道威廉·T·谢尔曼看见的那本书；那是灰胡子记日子的本子，上面印着他们叫作广告的东西。那家银行是想让人知道它有一百万块钱的本钱；这是我在算术课上学来的。灰胡子不是主；他是传教士——跟去非洲、去格陵兰冰山的那种是一路。”
